 succeeded in 127ms:
# Run a program with a ceiling (PC watchdog). Agent runs also stop when progress stalls (issue 818).
# The program's output goes to this window (so "tmo.bat ... > log.txt 2>&1" captures it).
# Call it through tools\pc\tmo.bat <seconds> <exe> [args ...]: the bat hands the whole command line over in TMO_ARGS, so
# arguments that start with "-" or "--" reach the program untouched (PowerShell would read them as its own parameters).
param([switch]$LibraryOnly)
$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'

# runner_stall_tests.js runs this decision with fake clocks on Linux CI and probes real processes on Windows.
function Get-StopReason($Elapsed, $Idle, $Ceiling, $StallMinutes) {
  if ($Elapsed -ge $Ceiling) { return 'CEILING' }
  if ($Idle -ge ($StallMinutes * 60)) { return "STALLED after $StallMinutes min" }
  return ''
}
function Get-RunProgress($Repo, $Logs) {
  $files = @{}
  $dirs = New-Object 'System.Collections.Generic.Stack[string]'
  $dirs.Push($Repo)
  while ($dirs.Count) {
    foreach ($f in (Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $dirs.Pop() -Force -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)) {
      # Reading git status may touch its index; Git housekeeping must not keep a silent agent alive.
      # Do not follow junctions outside this run's checkout.
      if ($f.Name -eq '.git' -or ($f.Attributes -band [IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint)) { continue }
      if ($f.PSIsContainer) { $dirs.Push($f.FullName) }
      else { $files[$f.FullName] = "$($f.LastWriteTimeUtc.Ticks):$($f.Length)" }
    }
  }
  $head = (& git.exe -C $Repo rev-parse --verify HEAD 2>$null) -join ''
  $bytes = @{}
  foreach ($path in $Logs) {
    $f = Get-Item -LiteralPath $path -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
    $bytes[$path] = if ($f) { $f.Length } else { 0 }
  }
  return @{ Files = $files; Head = $head; Bytes = $bytes }
}
function Test-RunProgress($Before, $After) {
  if ($After.Head -and $After.Head -ne $Before.Head) { return $true }
  if ($After.Files.Count -ne $Before.Files.Count) { return $true }
  foreach ($key in $After.Files.Keys) {
    if (-not $Before.Files.ContainsKey($key) -or $Before.Files[$key] -ne $After.Files[$key]) { return $true }
  }
  foreach ($key in $After.Bytes.Keys) {
    if ($After.Bytes[$key] -gt $Before.Bytes[$key]) { return $true }
  }
  return $false
}
function Save-RunResume($Repo, $Out, $Logs, $Reason, $Base) {
  $utf8 = New-Object Text.UTF8Encoding($false)
  $changed = @(& git.exe --no-optional-locks -C $Repo -c core.quotepath=off diff --name-only $Base -- 2>$null)
  $changed += @(& git.exe --no-optional-locks -C $Repo -c core.quotepath=off ls-files --others --exclude-standard 2>$null)
  $changed = @($changed | Sort-Object -Unique)
  $note = "## Runner resume note (issue 818)`nStop reason: $Reason`n`nChanged files (from run base $Base):`n"
  if ($changed.Count) { $note += ($changed | ForEach-Object { "- $_" }) -join "`n" }
  else { $note += '(none)' }
  foreach ($path in $Logs) {
    $note += "`n`nLast log lines ($([IO.Path]::GetFileName($path))):`n"
    if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $path) {
      $tail = @(Get-Content -LiteralPath $path -Tail 12 -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | ForEach-Object {
        $line = [string]$_; if ($line.Length -gt 400) { $line = $line.Substring(0, 400) + ' [cut]' }; $line
      })
      $note += $tail -join "`n"
    } else { $note += '(no output)' }
  }
  $note += "`n## End runner resume note`n"
  [IO.File]::WriteAllText((Join-Path $Out 'resume.txt'), $note, $utf8)
  [IO.File]::WriteAllText((Join-Path $Out 'stop_reason.txt'), $Reason, $utf8)
}
# taskkill /T uses process enumeration that can be denied in the Codex sandbox, even for our own children.
# Toolhelp needs no WMI access. Keep process handles, not just PIDs, so a reused PID can never target another job.
function Initialize-RunTree {
  if ('RunnerProcessSnapshot' -as [type]) { return }
  Add-Type -TypeDefinition @'
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.ComponentModel;
using System.Runtime.InteropServices;
public static class RunnerProcessSnapshot {
  [StructLayout(LayoutKind.Sequential, CharSet = CharSet.Unicode)]
  struct Entry {
    public uint size, usage, id; public UIntPtr heap; public uint module, threads, parent;
    public int priority; public uint flags;
    [MarshalAs(UnmanagedType.ByValTStr, SizeConst = 260)] public string exe;
  }
  [DllImport("kernel32.dll", SetLastError = true)] static extern IntPtr CreateToolhelp32Snapshot(uint flags, uint id);
  [DllImport("kernel32.dll", CharSet = CharSet.Unicode, SetLastError = true)] static extern bool Process32FirstW(IntPtr h, ref Entry e);
  [DllImport("kernel32.dll", CharSet = CharSet.Unicode, SetLastError = true)] static extern bool Process32NextW(IntPtr h, ref Entry e);
  [DllImport("kernel32.dll")] static extern bool CloseHandle(IntPtr h);
  public static Dictionary<int, int> Parents() {
    IntPtr h = CreateToolhelp32Snapshot(2, 0);
    if (h == new IntPtr(-1)) throw new Win32Exception();
    try {
      var result = new Dictionary<int, int>();
      var e = new Entry(); e.size = (uint)Marshal.SizeOf(typeof(Entry));
      if (!Process32FirstW(h, ref e)) throw new Win32Exception();
      do { result[(int)e.id] = (int)e.parent; } while (Process32NextW(h, ref e));
      if (Marshal.GetLastWin32Error() != 18) throw new Win32Exception();
      return result;
    } finally { CloseHandle(h); }
  }
}
'@
}
function Update-RunTree($Tree) {
  $parents = [RunnerProcessSnapshot]::Parents()
  do {
    $added = $false
    foreach ($childId in $parents.Keys) {
      $parentId = $parents[$childId]
      if ($Tree.ContainsKey($childId) -or -not $Tree.ContainsKey($parentId)) { continue }
      $parent = $Tree[$parentId]
      # A retained handle keeps the original process identity after exit. StartTime also rejects reused parent PIDs.
      try {
        $child = [Diagnostics.Process]::GetProcessById($childId)
        $null = $child.Handle
        if ($child.StartTime -lt $parent.StartTime) { $child.Dispose(); continue }
        if ($parent.HasExited -and $child.StartTime -gt $parent.ExitTime) { $child.Dispose(); continue }
        $Tree[$childId] = $child; $added = $true
      } catch [ArgumentException] { } # The child already exited between snapshot and open.
    }
  } while ($added)
}
function Write-KillLog($Log, $Line) {
  Write-Output "run_timeout: $Line"
  try { "$(Get-Date -Format s) $Line" | Out-File -LiteralPath $Log -Append -Encoding utf8 }
  catch { Write-Output "run_timeout: could not append kill log: $($_.Exception.Message)" }
}
function Stop-RunTree($Process, $Tree, $Log) {
  Update-RunTree $Tree
  $ea = $ErrorActionPreference
  $killer = 'taskkill.exe'
  if ($env:PC_RUNNER_TEST -eq '1' -and $env:PC_TEST_TASKKILL) { $killer = $env:PC_TEST_TASKKILL }
  try {
    $ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue'
    $reply = @(& $killer /PID $Process.Id /T /F 2>&1)
    $code = $LASTEXITCODE
  } finally { $ErrorActionPreference = $ea }
  Write-KillLog $Log "taskkill PID $($Process.Id) exit ${code}: $($reply -join ' ')"
  $deadline = [DateTime]::UtcNow.AddSeconds(15)
  do {
    # Discover children before stopping their parents, and repeat in case one spawned during taskkill.
    Update-RunTree $Tree
    $live = @($Tree.Values | Where-Object { -not $_.HasExited } | Sort-Object StartTime -Descending)
    foreach ($child in $live) {
      try { $child.Kill() }
      catch { if (-not $child.HasExited) { Write-KillLog $Log "handle kill PID $($child.Id) failed: $($_.Exception.Message)" } }
    }
    foreach ($child in $live) { $null = $child.WaitForExit(100) }
    Update-RunTree $Tree
    $live = @($Tree.Values | Where-Object { -not $_.HasExited })
    if (-not $live.Count) {
      Write-KillLog $Log "kill verified: all $($Tree.Count) processes gone (PIDs $((@($Tree.Keys) | Sort-Object) -join ', '))"
      return
    }
  } while ([DateTime]::UtcNow -lt $deadline)
  $failure = "KILL FAILED: surviving PIDs $(($live | ForEach-Object { $_.Id }) -join ', '); checkout kept"
  Write-KillLog $Log $failure
  throw $failure
}
if ($LibraryOnly) { return }
$raw = $env:TMO_ARGS
if (-not $raw) { Write-Output 'usage: tmo.bat <seconds> <exe> [args ...]'; exit 125 }
$m = [regex]::Match($raw, '^\s*(\d+)\s+("([^"]+)"|(\S+))\s*(.*)$')
if (-not $m.Success) { Write-Output "run_timeout: cannot read the command line: $raw"; exit 125 }
$Seconds = [int]$m.Groups[1].Value
$Exe = if ($m.Groups[3].Success) { $m.Groups[3].Value } else { $m.Groups[4].Value }
$argLine = $m.Groups[5].Value
$root = Join-Path $env:USERPROFILE 'Downloads\voidsworn_test'
$logDir = Join-Path $root 'queue\logs'
if (-not (Test-Path $logDir)) { $logDir = $PSScriptRoot }
$log = Join-Path $logDir 'timeouts.log'
if ($env:PC_RUNNER_TEST -eq '1' -and $env:PC_TEST_TIMEOUT_LOG) { $log = $env:PC_TEST_TIMEOUT_LOG }
$watch = $env:TMO_REPO -and $env:TMO_OUT -and $env:TMO_LOG
$watchRepo = $env:TMO_REPO; $watchOut = $env:TMO_OUT
$stall = 30.0
if ($watch -and $env:STALL_MIN) { $stall = [double]::Parse($env:STALL_MIN, [Globalization.CultureInfo]::InvariantCulture) }
if ($Seconds -le 0 -or $stall -le 0 -or [double]::IsNaN($stall) -or [double]::IsInfinity($stall)) { throw 'ceiling and STALL_MIN must be positive' }
$logs = @($env:TMO_LOG, $env:TMO_ERR_LOG) | Where-Object { $_ }
if ($watch) {
  $before = Get-RunProgress $watchRepo $logs
  $base = $before.Head
  $bp = Join-Path $watchOut 'base_commit.txt'
  if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $bp) { $base = [IO.File]::ReadAllText($bp).Trim() }
}
# Nov 1: a test job ran all through Ryan's first Godot playtest, slowing the PC and overwriting the game's log.
# Wait while Ryan is playing: the game is Voidsworn.exe or a windowed Godot started with no job arguments
# (play_godot.bat passes none; every job passes --headless, --script, --bot or similar). Waiting is not timed.
function Test-RyanPlaying {
  try { $ps = Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "Name LIKE 'Voidsworn%.exe' OR Name LIKE 'Godot%.exe'" } catch { return $false }
  foreach ($q in $ps) {
    if ($q.Name -like '*_console.exe') { continue }
    $cl = [string]$q.CommandLine
    $rest = [regex]::Replace($cl, '^\s*("[^"]*"|\S+)', '')
    $rest = [regex]::Replace($rest, '--path\s+("[^"]*"|\S+)', '')
    if ($rest -notmatch '--?\w') { return $true }
  }
  return $false
}
$w0 = Get-Date; $said = $false
# Codex jobs are light (one model session, small node scripts) and do not touch the game's log: they run during play.
$noWait = ($Exe -match 'codex(\.cmd|\.exe)?$') -or ($env:PC_RUNNER_TEST -eq '1')
while ((-not $noWait) -and (Test-RyanPlaying) -and ((Get-Date) - $w0).TotalHours -lt 8) {
  if (-not $said) { Write-Output "run_timeout: the game is open, waiting until it closes"; "$(Get-Date -Format s) WAIT game open: $Exe" | Out-File $log -Append -Encoding utf8; $said = $true }
  Start-Sleep -Seconds 20
}
if ($said) { "$(Get-Date -Format s) RESUME after $([int]((Get-Date) - $w0).TotalMinutes) min: $Exe" | Out-File $log -Append -Encoding utf8 }
# R293: acquire only around the GPU command, never the whole model session.
# A pending solo claim drains all shared slots and blocks new workers.
$gpuHelper = Join-Path $PSScriptRoot 'gpu_slots.js'
$gpuNode = 'node.exe'
$bundledNode = Join-Path $root 'node\node.exe'
if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $bundledNode) { $gpuNode = $bundledNode }
$gpuRoot = $root
if ($env:PC_RUNNER_TEST -eq '1' -and $env:PC_TEST_GPU_ROOT) { $gpuRoot = $env:PC_TEST_GPU_ROOT }
$gpuToken = ''
try {
$classify = @{ exe = $Exe; args = $argLine; cwd = (Get-Location).Path; requested = [string]$env:PC_GPU_MODE; weight = [string]$env:PC_GPU_WEIGHT } | ConvertTo-Json -Compress
$gpuClass = ($classify | & $gpuNode $gpuHelper classify) -join ''
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'GPU command classification failed' }
$gpuClass = $gpuClass | ConvertFrom-Json
$gpuMode = $gpuClass.mode
if ($gpuMode) {
  Write-Output "GPU: waiting for $gpuMode claim, weight $($gpuClass.weight) (issue $env:PC_GPU_ISSUE)"
  # Admission has its own clock in gpu_slots.js (PC_GPU_WAIT_SECONDS, default 300).
  # Read the helper's diagnostic even under Windows PowerShell's native stderr handling.
  $ea = $ErrorActionPreference
  try {
    $ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue'
    & $gpuNode $gpuHelper acquire $gpuRoot ([string]$env:PC_GPU_ISSUE + ':gpu') $PID $gpuMode $gpuClass.weight 2>&1 | ForEach-Object {
      $line = [string]$_
      if ($line -eq 'WAIT') { Write-Host "GPU: still waiting for $gpuMode claim" }
      elseif ($line -cmatch '\A[0-9a-f]{8}(?:-[0-9a-f]{4}){3}-[0-9a-f]{12}\z') { $gpuToken = $line }
      else { Write-Output $line }
    }
    $admissionExit = $LASTEXITCODE
  } finally { $ErrorActionPreference = $ea }
  if ($admissionExit -ne 0 -or -not $gpuToken) {
    Write-Output 'GPU: admission failed; command was not started'
    exit 126
  }
  Write-Output "GPU: acquired $gpuMode claim $gpuToken"
}
# A forced claim applies to this command only, never a nested watchdog.
$env:PC_GPU_MODE = ''
$env:PC_GPU_WEIGHT = ''
Initialize-RunTree
$timer = [Diagnostics.Stopwatch]::StartNew()
$lastProgress = 0.0
$scanEvery = 60.0
if ($env:PC_RUNNER_TEST -eq '1' -and $env:PC_TEST_SCAN_S) { $scanEvery = [double]$env:PC_TEST_SCAN_S }
$nextScan = $scanEvery
# Child commands may use tmo.bat too. Their own timeout must never overwrite this agent's resume note.
$env:TMO_REPO = ''; $env:TMO_OUT = ''; $env:TMO_LOG = ''; $env:TMO_ERR_LOG = ''
try {
  if ($argLine -ne '') { $p = Start-Process -FilePath $Exe -ArgumentList $argLine -NoNewWindow -PassThru }
  else { $p = Start-Process -FilePath $Exe -NoNewWindow -PassThru }
} catch {
  "$(Get-Date -Format s) START FAILED $Exe $argLine : $_" | Out-File $log -Append -Encoding utf8
  Write-Output "run_timeout: could not start $Exe : $_"
  exit 125
}
$null = $p.Handle   # keeps the exit code readable after the process ends
$tree = @{ }; $tree[$p.Id] = $p
$completed = $false
try {
$poll = 5000
if ($env:PC_RUNNER_TEST -eq '1' -and $env:PC_TEST_POLL_MS) { $poll = [int]$env:PC_TEST_POLL_MS }
while ($true) {
  Update-RunTree $tree
  $remaining = [Math]::Max(1, [Math]::Min($poll, ($Seconds - $timer.Elapsed.TotalSeconds) * 1000))
  if ($p.WaitForExit([int]$remaining)) { $p.WaitForExit(); $completed = $true; exit $p.ExitCode }
  if ($watch) {
    # Scan the checkout once a minute, not every poll: a full walk of a big checkout every 5 s would load Ryan's PC for hours,
    # and a 30 minute stall window does not need finer steps (Board review, Nov 15).
    if ($timer.Elapsed.TotalSeconds -ge $nextScan) {
      $after = Get-RunProgress $watchRepo $logs
      if (Test-RunProgress $before $after) { $lastProgress = $timer.Elapsed.TotalSeconds }
      $before = $after
      $nextScan = $timer.Elapsed.TotalSeconds + $scanEvery
    }
    $reason = Get-StopReason $timer.Elapsed.TotalSeconds ($timer.Elapsed.TotalSeconds - $lastProgress) $Seconds $stall
  } else {
    $reason = if ($timer.Elapsed.TotalSeconds -ge $Seconds) { 'CEILING' } else { '' }
  }
  if ($p.HasExited) { $p.WaitForExit(); $completed = $true; exit $p.ExitCode }
  if ($reason) { break }
}
$secs = [int]$timer.Elapsed.TotalSeconds
Stop-RunTree $p $tree $log
if ($watch) { Save-RunResume $watchRepo $watchOut $logs $reason $base }
"$(Get-Date -Format s) TIMEOUT $reason after $secs s (ceiling $Seconds s), verified dead tree of PID $($p.Id): $Exe $argLine" | Out-File $log -Append -Encoding utf8
Write-Output "run_timeout: TIMEOUT $reason after $secs s, killed $Exe (PID $($p.Id)) and its children"
exit 124
} finally {
  # Reap on an unexpected watchdog error too. A successful queue launcher may deliberately start background jobs.
  if (-not $completed) {
    Update-RunTree $tree
    if (@($tree.Values | Where-Object { -not $_.HasExited }).Count) { Stop-RunTree $p $tree $log }
  }
  foreach ($child in $tree.Values) { $child.Dispose() }
}
} finally {
  if ($gpuToken) {
    & $gpuNode $gpuHelper release $gpuRoot $gpuToken
    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { Write-Output "GPU: release failed; dead owner PID $PID will be reclaimed" }
  }
}
@echo off
rem Run a program with a time limit: tmo.bat <seconds> <exe> [args ...]
rem Kills the whole process tree if it overruns (exit code 124, logged to Downloads\voidsworn_test\queue\logs\timeouts.log);
rem otherwise returns the program's own exit code. Its output comes out here, so "tmo.bat ... > log.txt 2>&1" captures it.
rem For agent runs set TMO_REPO, TMO_OUT and TMO_LOG. The seconds argument is then the hard ceiling (runners default
rem to 21600), and STALL_MIN (default 30) stops a run only after no file, commit or log progress. TMO_ERR_LOG is optional.
rem A stopped agent writes resume.txt and stop_reason.txt in TMO_OUT. Clear these TMO variables before runner checks.
rem R293: shared capacity is in Downloads\voidsworn_test\gpu_slots.json, e.g. {"sharedSlots":4}, default 2.
rem Godot GPU commands weigh 1, Blender commands weigh 2; PC_GPU_WEIGHT overrides the weight for this command.
rem Timing scripts take solo and wait for all slots, whatever the shared capacity.
rem For other GPU executables set PC_GPU_MODE=shared, or solo for timing. Waiting does not spend the command's limit.
setlocal
set "TMO_ARGS=%*"
powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "%~dp0run_timeout.ps1"
exit /b %ERRORLEVEL%

 succeeded in 136ms:
# The Fable program: one finished battle, and playbooks other models can run

Issue 405. FINDINGS R167. Written by the Board (Fable 5.2), Nov 10 2026.

## What Ryan asked for

"I still have another full fable reset, so we can use fable on the really key stuff. Design, graphics, effects, etc. let's do
everything we can to use the fable resets smart here. Playbooks or recipes, deep reviews and redesign, etc. codebase is yours
with my direction and vision, you bring it to life with the decisions needed from me. I want this to end with something that
opus or other models can run from" (Nov 10 2026, 08:42 CST).

His scope answers:
- Focus, in this order of weight: battle effects, ships, space and the board. Screens and sound still get a playbook, lighter.
- End state: "One finished battle + playbooks".
- Steering: "Options first, then build". He sees two or three options, picks one, then it is built.
- Decisions: "As they come". Two lines and one image, whenever a pick is ready.

## The end state

1. **One finished battle.** A single fight that looks and reads final from the first shot to the last wreck, at the camera the
   game is really played at (camera 170 on the look bench). It is the proof that the recipes work and the reference every
   later job is judged against.
2. **A playbook per area**, written from what that battle needed, so another model can do the next ship, effect, hex or
   screen without asking: docs/playbooks/effects.md, ships.md, space.md, screens.md, sound.md.
3. **The front door**: HANDOFF.md section 0 and AGENTS.md point at the playbooks, and the job board holds the remaining items
   as ready jobs written from each playbook's job template.

## The finished battle (what "final" means here)

- **Board**: the dead-star gravity-well board (owner ruling, Oct 24).
- **Fleets**: about six to eight ships a side. **Sunward Crusade against Eclipse** (Ryan, Nov 11 2026, R180; this replaces the
  first plan of the Hullbreakers plus one other). Both have a settled flagship (docs/playbooks/factions.md) and look nothing
  alike. Each fleet needs one capital, one cruiser or destroyer and one escort through the ship recipe.
- **Ships**: built by tools/recipe with real thickness and comprehensive insides (R98, R209, R215), hard points by hull class
  and faction theme (R164 item 4, issue 363), from an accepted concept in the one style (R197) and an Opus build sheet that
  passes the concept standard (R203, R205, R207; docs/playbooks/ships.md).
- **Effects**: every big moment staged to Muse's table (contrib/muse/design/hit_staging_2026_11/synthesis.md): explosions by
  the picked route (issues 353, 365), deaths in three beats (366), one kit of trails, beams and flares (367), effects that
  start on the ship (370), shields that hug the hull with one shield pool, per-side coverage and lossy power routing, resting as Rim and Gap (R246 replaces R163 item 2's four strengths and invisible rest; 371 rules first, then 372 the look; design in contrib/muse/design/shields_2026_11/).
- **Space**: nebula and ion storm hexes with depth (368), a board planet and rocks from real data, remixed past recognition
  (R163 item 3; 369), the black hole and sky calm enough that ships and effects win.
- **Screen**: the battle screen in the ember orders-sheet look, layout E1 Ledger, shield readouts in Glacier #92b8e8 (Ryan,
  Nov 11: "Retire lime", R191; "E1 Ledger" and "Glacier pale blue", R210). Being built: issue 271 step 2.
- **Sound**: scoped (R171: weapons, hits and deaths, buttons and menus, ambience, music; "Clean and tactical"). Routing is in
  (issue 506); sound weight is next (R193 (4)); the redo follows the listening page.
- **Done**: a capture of the whole fight and its look bench sheet, and Ryan says yes.

## How every item flows

1. **Options.** Fable makes two or three options: a bench sheet of real captures where the thing exists, a flat mockup where
   it does not. Cheap, fast, and never more than one sheet per decision.
2. **Pick.** Ryan picks, with one image and two lines in front of him. The pick is recorded in FINDINGS with his words.
3. **Build.** The seat the table below names builds it. One worked example first, never the whole set.
4. **Proof.** The look bench before and after, at camera 170 first (bench a commit twice, keep the second run), plus the
   area's own check. Fable looks at the pictures, not the claim.
5. **Recipe.** What worked is written into the area's playbook the same day: target, steps, check, traps, job template.
6. **Roll out.** The rest of that kind (the other 34 hulls, the other weapons, the other hexes) becomes jobs written from the
   playbook's template, for DeepSeek, Codex or Opus. Fable only reviews their pictures.

Standing rules for every flow (Nov 16 2026, issue 880): the run brief lives in the issue body, one current instruction
(R281); an open issue is not proof of undone work, and a merge closes its issue in the same step when its scope is done
(R288); every picture carries its revision and stage; and visual work is compared with its concept and ruling, with the
misses stated plainly, before it merges (RULEBOOK 2.3 and 2.4).

building" (R202). And 17:45 and 17:48 CST: "I think builds in blender should come from Opus... Codex and deepseek should do
other work to free you up on these crucial items" (R205, R206). So "Fable" in the track tables below reads as the Board

Downloads\voidsworn_test, clone from disk, retry failed clone/fetch steps three times total with two-minute gaps, and make

## The playbook format (every playbook, same headings)

1. **Target**: the pictures to match and Ryan's own words about them.
2. **Inputs**: what must exist before you start.
3. **Steps**: numbered, with the exact job files and commands.
4. **The check that proves it**: bench rows, cameras, numbers, and what a pass looks like.
5. **Traps**: what went wrong before, each with its FINDINGS row.
6. **Worked example**: the pull requests that did it once.
7. **Job template**: the issue block to copy for the next item of this kind.
8. **Seat**: who runs it, and when to hand a picture back to the Board.

A playbook is done when a model that has never seen this repo ships the next item from it and the Board changes nothing.

## Budget and pacing (rewritten Nov 11 2026 evening)

- Ryan's Fable resets are all used (R180). The Board and Opus workers draw the Claude plan: two Opus workers at most while the
  Board thread is active (the five-hour limit was hit on Nov 11 with three; LOOP.md duties item 9).
- Codex gives about 100 minutes of work per five-hour window; one Codex job per lane; GPU work sized to the job (RULEBOOK 3.4, R293).
- If a limit is hit, nothing stops: DeepSeek, Muse and Codex keep running the rows that are theirs, and the Board's picks wait
  in LOOP.md.

@echo off
rem Issue 1174: the one shared ship build job. Every helper's ship run is a three-line wrapper that sets the inputs and calls
rem this file (docs/ship_pipeline/ship_job/README.md). Built from issue 1157's S10_RUN_1157.bat, the best guarded job of Nov 19.
rem
rem Inputs (environment, set by the wrapper):
rem   SHIP        the recipe ship id, the file name in tools\recipe\ships without .json (voidsworn_S10)
rem   CODE        the game folder the install writes (V16_37)
rem   BRANCH      the branch to build (opus/issue-1157)
rem   JOB_DIR     this job's own folder under Downloads\voidsworn_test (opus1157)
rem   STAGES      optional: the recipe stages, in order (default 1_concept_in to 7_export, all seven)
rem   SETS        optional: the a4_shots sets (default battle,close)
rem   FACS        optional: the a4_shots faction (default the part of SHIP before its first underscore)
rem   PAINT_BASE  optional: a branch merged in after the checkout; the pictures are then a labelled PREVIEW of that stack
rem   PC_GPU_WAIT_SECONDS  optional: how long each GPU step waits for its claim (default 3600, the most gpu_slots.js
rem               allows; the old 300 s default failed 1155, 1157 and 1158 on Nov 19 when several helpers queued at once)
rem
rem What it does, in order. Any failure skips every later step, the checks and the shots (the recipe folder is still copied
rem back, step 7, for the logs), and the last two lines of out\result.txt are always "done PASS" or "done FAILED: <step>" and then "all done".
rem   1. runs from its own copy in JOB_DIR (cmd reads a .bat as it goes, so a checkout must never rewrite the running file)
rem   2. its own clone JOB_DIR\repo (depth 50), the branch checked out (install files restored after a
rem      saved diff, never a forced checkout; any other local change or file collision stops the job), PAINT_BASE merged if set, a commit= line
rem   3. the headless import, then each stage logged with its exit code (stage 7 installs into the clone's own godot folder,
rem      never into Ryan's game)
rem   4. the installed glb must be this run's (written after the run started), else the install counts as failed
rem   5. the GPU readback, the authored interiors check and the death seam check on the installed glb; all three must pass
rem   6. the in-game shots at camera 170 and 60, only when everything above passed
rem   7. the recipe folder copied (never moved) into out\recipe
rem Writes only under voidsworn_test\JOB_DIR and voidsworn_test\recipe\SHIP (the stages' own folder). Deletes nothing: an earlier
rem run's out folder is renamed out_before_<time>, never removed.
if "%~1"=="ship" goto ship
setlocal EnableExtensions
set "V=%USERPROFILE%\Downloads\voidsworn_test"
set MISSING=
if not defined SHIP set MISSING=SHIP
if not defined CODE set MISSING=%MISSING% CODE
if not defined BRANCH set MISSING=%MISSING% BRANCH
if not defined JOB_DIR set MISSING=%MISSING% JOB_DIR
if defined MISSING (
  echo ship_job.bat: set %MISSING% first. See docs\ship_pipeline\ship_job\README.md for the three-line wrapper.
  exit /b 2
)
set "J=%V%\%JOB_DIR%"
if not exist "%J%" mkdir "%J%"
rem 1. run from this job's own copy
if /i not "%~f0"=="%J%\ship_job_running.bat" (
  copy /y "%~f0" "%J%\ship_job_running.bat" > nul
  call "%J%\ship_job_running.bat"
  exit /b
)
if not defined STAGES set "STAGES=1_concept_in 2_shape_ref 3_trace_hull 4_parts_kit 5_paint_bake 6_lod 7_export"
if not defined SETS set "SETS=battle,close"
if not defined FACS for /f "delims=_" %%F in ("%SHIP%") do set "FACS=%%F"
if not defined PC_GPU_WAIT_SECONDS set PC_GPU_WAIT_SECONDS=3600
if not defined PC_GPU_ISSUE set "PC_GPU_ISSUE=%JOB_DIR%"
set "C=%J%\repo"
set "O=%J%\out"
for /f "usebackq delims=" %%T in (`powershell -NoProfile -Command "(Get-Date).ToString('yyyyMMdd_HHmmss')"`) do set "STAMP=%%T"
if exist "%O%" ren "%O%" "out_before_%STAMP%"
mkdir "%O%"
set "RES=%O%\result.txt"
for /f "usebackq delims=" %%T in (`powershell -NoProfile -Command "[DateTime]::UtcNow.Ticks"`) do set "START_TICKS=%%T"
echo start %SHIP% %CODE% %BRANCH% %DATE% %TIME% > "%RES%"
echo inputs STAGES=%STAGES% SETS=%SETS% FACS=%FACS% PAINT_BASE=%PAINT_BASE% PC_GPU_WAIT_SECONDS=%PC_GPU_WAIT_SECONDS% >> "%RES%"
if exist "%J%\out_before_%STAMP%" echo the earlier run's out folder is now out_before_%STAMP% >> "%RES%"
set FAILED=
set "GD=%V%\godot\Godot_v4.7.2-stable_win64_console.exe"

rem 2. own clone, the branch, PAINT_BASE
if not exist "%C%\.git" git clone -q --depth 50 https://github.com/rcbailey12/voidsworn "%C%" >> "%RES%" 2>&1
git -C "%C%" fetch -q --depth 50 origin %BRANCH% >> "%RES%" 2>&1
if errorlevel 1 set "FAILED=fetch %BRANCH%"
if defined FAILED goto collect
rem the checkout, in this job's own clone: stage 7 rewrites the tracked godot\assets\ships files here (manifest.json, the CODE
rem folder), and a plain checkout then refuses every rerun after the branch moved. So: list and save every tracked change
rem (clone_changes_before_checkout.txt and .patch); stop on a tracked change anywhere else (a hand fix in the clone); restore
rem only godot\assets\ships from the clone's own commit; then a plain checkout, never forced, that also refuses to overwrite
rem untracked or ignored files, so a local file the branch now tracks stops the job instead of being replaced (Artoo, PR 1179).
git -C "%C%" status --porcelain --untracked-files=no > "%O%\clone_changes_before_checkout.txt" 2> "%O%\log_clone_status.txt"
git -C "%C%" diff --binary HEAD > "%O%\clone_changes_before_checkout.patch" 2>> "%O%\log_clone_status.txt"
findstr /v /r /c:"^.. godot/assets/ships/" "%O%\clone_changes_before_checkout.txt" > "%O%\clone_changes_unexpected.txt"
for %%Z in ("%O%\clone_changes_unexpected.txt") do if %%~zZ GTR 0 set "FAILED=checkout: the clone has local changes outside godot\assets\ships, listed in clone_changes_unexpected.txt and saved in clone_changes_before_checkout.patch; nothing was reset"
if defined FAILED goto collect
for %%Z in ("%O%\clone_changes_before_checkout.txt") do if %%~zZ GTR 0 echo restoring the clone's install files listed in clone_changes_before_checkout.txt, saved first in clone_changes_before_checkout.patch >> "%RES%"
git -C "%C%" checkout -q HEAD -- godot/assets/ships >> "%RES%" 2>&1
if errorlevel 1 set "FAILED=checkout: could not restore godot\assets\ships in the clone"
if defined FAILED goto collect
git -C "%C%" checkout -q --no-overwrite-ignore -B shipjob FETCH_HEAD >> "%RES%" 2>&1
if errorlevel 1 set "FAILED=checkout %BRANCH%"
if defined FAILED goto collect
if not defined PAINT_BASE goto based
git -C "%C%" fetch -q --depth 50 origin %PAINT_BASE% >> "%RES%" 2>&1
if errorlevel 1 set "FAILED=fetch PAINT_BASE %PAINT_BASE%"
if defined FAILED goto collect
git -C "%C%" -c user.name=%JOB_DIR% -c user.email=%JOB_DIR%@localhost merge -q --no-edit --no-overwrite-ignore FETCH_HEAD >> "%RES%" 2>&1
if errorlevel 1 set "FAILED=merge PAINT_BASE %PAINT_BASE%"
if defined FAILED git -C "%C%" merge --abort >> "%RES%" 2>&1
if defined FAILED goto collect
echo merged %PAINT_BASE%: the pictures of this run are a PREVIEW of that paint stack >> "%RES%"
:based
git -C "%C%" log -1 --format=commit=%%H >> "%RES%" 2>&1

rem 3. import, then the stages, each in a fresh cmd so the variables the stages set end with them
cd /d "%C%"
call "%C%\tools\pc\tmo.bat" 21600 "%GD%" --headless --path "%C%\godot" --import > "%O%\log_import.txt" 2>&1
set EL=%ERRORLEVEL%
echo import exit %EL% >> "%RES%"
if not "%EL%"=="0" set "FAILED=import"
if defined FAILED goto collect
cmd /c ""%~f0" ship"
set EL=%ERRORLEVEL%
echo stages exit %EL% >> "%RES%"
if not "%EL%"=="0" set "FAILED=a stage (see the SKIPPED line above)"
if defined FAILED goto collect
echo %STAGES% | findstr /c:"7_export" > nul
if errorlevel 1 (
  echo checks and shots SKIPPED: STAGES has no 7_export, so nothing was installed >> "%RES%"
  goto collect
)

rem 4. the installed glb must be this run's
set "GLB=%C%\godot\assets\ships\%CODE%\%CODE%.glb"
powershell -NoProfile -Command "$f = Get-Item -LiteralPath $env:GLB -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue; if (-not $f -or $f.LastWriteTimeUtc.Ticks -lt [long]$env:START_TICKS) { exit 1 }"
if errorlevel 1 set "FAILED=install (%CODE%.glb missing or older than this run)"
if defined FAILED goto collect
echo install fresh: %GLB% >> "%RES%"

rem 5. the three checks on the installed glb; every one must pass
set CHECKFAIL=
call "%C%\tools\pc\tmo.bat" 21600 "%GD%" --path "%C%\godot" --script res://render/test/installed_ship_check.gd -- %CODE% > "%O%\log_readback_gpu.txt" 2>&1
set EL=%ERRORLEVEL%
echo gpu readback exit %EL% >> "%RES%"
if not "%EL%"=="0" set "CHECKFAIL=%CHECKFAIL% readback"
call "%C%\tools\pc\tmo.bat" 21600 "%GD%" --headless --path "%C%\godot" --script res://fx/test/authored_interiors_check.gd -- "%GLB%" > "%O%\log_interiors.txt" 2>&1
set EL=%ERRORLEVEL%
echo authored interiors exit %EL% >> "%RES%"
if not "%EL%"=="0" set "CHECKFAIL=%CHECKFAIL% interiors"
call "%C%\tools\pc\tmo.bat" 21600 "%GD%" --headless --path "%C%\godot" --script res://fx/test/death_seam_check.gd -- "%GLB%" > "%O%\log_death_seam.txt" 2>&1
set EL=%ERRORLEVEL%
echo death seam exit %EL% >> "%RES%"
if not "%EL%"=="0" set "CHECKFAIL=%CHECKFAIL% death_seam"
if defined CHECKFAIL set "FAILED=checks:%CHECKFAIL%"
if defined FAILED goto collect

rem 6. the shots, only now: everything above passed, so the installed glb is this run's and checked
call "%C%\tools\pc\tmo.bat" 21600 "%GD%" --path "%C%\godot" --resolution 1920x1080 res://assets/ships/test/a4_shots.tscn -- --out "%O%\shots" --facs %FACS% --sets %SETS% --codes %CODE% > "%O%\log_shots.txt" 2>&1
set EL=%ERRORLEVEL%
echo shots exit %EL% >> "%RES%"
if not "%EL%"=="0" set "FAILED=shots"

rem 7. copy (never move) the recipe folder into out\recipe, so the Board can bring it back (R334)
:collect
if not exist "%V%\recipe\%SHIP%" (
  echo collect: no recipe\%SHIP% folder >> "%RES%"
  if not defined FAILED set "FAILED=collect (no recipe\%SHIP% folder)"
  goto finish
)
xcopy /e /i /y /q "%V%\recipe\%SHIP%" "%O%\recipe" > "%O%\log_collect.txt" 2>&1
set EL=%ERRORLEVEL%
echo collect exit %EL% >> "%RES%"
if not "%EL%"=="0" if not defined FAILED set "FAILED=collect"
echo %STAGES% | findstr /c:"7_export" > nul
if errorlevel 1 goto finish
if not exist "%O%\recipe\export\%CODE%\%CODE%.glb" if not defined FAILED set "FAILED=collect (out\recipe\export\%CODE%\%CODE%.glb missing)"

:finish
rem no parenthesised block here: FAILED can hold a ")" that would end the block early
if defined FAILED goto finish_failed
echo done PASS %DATE% %TIME% >> "%RES%"
echo all done >> "%RES%"
exit /b 0
:finish_failed
echo done FAILED: %FAILED% %DATE% %TIME% >> "%RES%"
echo all done >> "%RES%"
exit /b 1

:ship
rem the stages, in their own cmd (called above as: this file ship); SHIP, C, O, RES and STAGES come from the caller
set RECIPE_NOPAUSE=1
set "INSTALL_TO=%C%\godot"
set CAMJ=
set FAILED=
for %%S in (%STAGES%) do call :stage %%S
if defined FAILED (
  echo checks and shots SKIPPED: stage %FAILED% failed, so the installed glb is not this run's >> "%RES%"
  exit /b 1
)
exit /b 0

:stage
if defined FAILED (
  echo %1 SKIPPED: stage %FAILED% failed >> "%RES%"
  exit /b 0
)
echo %1 start %TIME% >> "%RES%"
call "%C%\tools\recipe\%1.bat" > "%O%\log_%1.txt" 2>&1
set EL=%ERRORLEVEL%
echo %1 exit %EL% at %TIME% >> "%RES%"
if not "%EL%"=="0" set FAILED=%1
exit /b 0

'use strict';
// R293: shared capacity by load, or one solo measurement. Node 22, no packages.
// All tools copies read ROOT/gpu_slots.json: { "sharedSlots": 2 } (missing = 2).
// The watchdog owns the claim, so killing its tree leaves a reclaimable dead PID.
const fs = require('fs');
const path = require('path');
const net = require('net');
const crypto = require('crypto');
const sleep = ms => new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, ms));
const slotName = index => 'gpu_slot_' + index;
const realClock = { now: () => performance.now(), sleep };
// The longest admission wait. ship_job.bat defaults to it (issue 1174): on Nov 19 three helpers queued at once and the
// 300 s default failed them. Ship jobs run the helper from their own branch checkout, so raising this only helps once every
// ship branch carries the change.
const MAX_WAIT_SECONDS = 3600;
function positiveInteger(value, label) {
  const number = Number(value);
  if (!/^[0-9]+$/.test(String(value)) || !Number.isSafeInteger(number) || number < 1) {
    throw new Error(label + ' must be a positive integer');
  }
  return number;
}
const alive = pid => {
  if (!Number.isInteger(pid) || pid <= 0) return false;
  try { process.kill(pid, 0); return true; } catch (e) { return e.code === 'EPERM'; }
};

// An OS-owned localhost bind serializes short file transactions across ALL tools
// copies. The OS drops it after a crash. No lock or job files are ever deleted.
// No client connects and no data is sent. An occupied port fails closed (waits).
// clock: { now, sleep } (tests pass a fake one, so no test races the wall clock; issue 1174). When the deadline has passed,
// check() runs once more before the lock's own message: an admission that runs out while the lock is busy reports
// "admission timed out", whichever of the two clocks reads first (the Nov 19 CI flake at gpu_slots_tests.js:159).
async function transaction(fn, check = () => {}, deadline, clock = realClock) {
  if (deadline === undefined) deadline = clock.now() + 5000;
  for (;;) {
    check();
    if (clock.now() >= deadline) { check(); throw new Error('GPU slot lock timed out'); }
    const server = net.createServer(socket => socket.destroy());
    const bound = await new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
      server.once('error', e => e.code === 'EADDRINUSE' ? resolve(false) : reject(e));
      server.listen({ host: '127.0.0.1', port: 47847, exclusive: true }, () => resolve(true));
    });
    if (!bound) { await clock.sleep(25); continue; }
    try { check(); return fn(); } finally { await new Promise(resolve => server.close(resolve)); }
  }
}

class Slots {
  constructor(root, isAlive = alive) { this.root = root; this.isAlive = isAlive; }
  capacity() {
    const config = this.read('gpu_slots.json');
    // Only a missing file uses the default. A damaged setting fails closed.
    if (config === null && !fs.existsSync(path.join(this.root, 'gpu_slots.json'))) return 2;
    return positiveInteger(config?.sharedSlots, 'gpu_slots.json sharedSlots');
  }
  names() {
    try { return fs.readdirSync(this.root).filter(name => /^gpu_slot_[1-9][0-9]*$/.test(name)).concat('gpu_solo'); }
    catch (e) { if (e.code === 'ENOENT') return ['gpu_solo']; throw e; }
  }
  read(name) {
    try {
      const text = fs.readFileSync(path.join(this.root, name), 'utf8').trim();
      return text ? JSON.parse(text) : null;
    } catch (e) {
      if (e.code === 'ENOENT') return null;
      // A damaged holder file must never silently grant another worker a slot.
      throw e;
    }
  }
  write(name, value) {
    fs.mkdirSync(this.root, { recursive: true });
    fs.writeFileSync(path.join(this.root, name), value ? JSON.stringify(value) + '\n' : '');
  }
  cleared() {
    try { return /^GPU cleared(?:\s*:.*)?$/i.test(fs.readFileSync(path.join(this.root, 'gpu_go.txt'), 'utf8').split(/\r?\n/)[0].trim()); }
    catch (e) { if (e.code === 'ENOENT') return false; throw e; }
  }
  checkWeight(holder) {
    const capacity = this.capacity();
    const weight = positiveInteger(holder.weight ?? 1, 'GPU weight');
    if (holder.mode === 'shared' && weight > capacity) throw new Error(`GPU weight ${weight} exceeds shared capacity ${capacity}`);
    return { capacity, weight };
  }
  step(holder, waiterPid = holder.pid) {
    const { capacity, weight } = this.checkWeight(holder);
    const state = this.names().map(name => {
      const item = this.read(name);
      if (item && !this.isAlive(item.pid)) { this.write(name, null); return null; }
      return item;
    });
    if (!this.isAlive(holder.pid)) throw new Error('GPU admission cancelled: owner exited');
    if (!this.cleared()) return null;
    // Include claims above a lowered capacity until they exit or are reclaimed.
    const occupied = state.slice(0, -1).filter(Boolean);
    const pending = state[state.length - 1];
    if (holder.mode === 'solo') {
      if (pending && pending.token !== holder.token) return null;
      // Reserve priority before draining existing shared workers. New workers wait.
      // While draining, the waiting helper owns the reservation. If it is killed
      // outright, the next caller can reclaim it even if the watchdog is alive.
      if (!pending) this.write('gpu_solo', { ...holder, pid: waiterPid });
      if (occupied.length) return null;
      this.write('gpu_slot_1', holder);
      this.write('gpu_solo', holder);
      return holder;
    }
    if (pending || occupied.some(item => item && item.mode === 'solo')) return null;
    if (occupied.length + weight > capacity) return null;
    // One holder occupies every unit of its weight, with admission serialized.
    // Keep the existing file names so claims survive tools updates.
    const free = [];
    for (let index = 1; free.length < weight; index++) {
      const name = slotName(index);
      if (!this.read(name)) free.push(name);
    }
    for (const name of free) this.write(name, holder);
    return holder;
  }
  release(token) {
    for (const name of this.names()) if (this.read(name)?.token === token) this.write(name, null);
  }
}

function weightFor(exe, requested = '') {
  if (requested !== '') return positiveInteger(requested, 'PC_GPU_WEIGHT');
  return /^blender(?:\.exe)?$/i.test(path.win32.basename(exe)) ? 2 : 1;
}

// Benchmarks take solo themselves, including direct tmo launches. Follow scene
// and script resources, so a scene's script cannot hide timing behind a .tscn.
function modeFor(exe, args, cwd = process.cwd(), requested = '') {
  const godot = /^(?:Godot|Voidsworn).*\.exe$/i.test(path.win32.basename(exe)) || /^godot(?:[34])?$/i.test(path.basename(exe));
  const blender = /^blender(?:\.exe)?$/i.test(path.win32.basename(exe));
  if (!godot && !blender) return requested === 'solo' || requested === 'shared' ? requested : '';
  if (godot && /(?:^|\s)--headless(?:\s|$)/i.test(args)) return '';
  if (requested === 'solo') return 'solo';
  if (blender) return 'shared';
  // Known benches remain solo even if their checkout has not been prepared yet.
  if (/--(?:bench|benchmark|measure)\b|bench209|scene_gate|bench\d|frame[_-]?time|performance|perf_probe/i.test(args) && !/--still\b/.test(args)) return 'solo';
  const project = /--path\s+(?:"([^"]+)"|(\S+))/.exec(args);
  const base = project ? path.resolve(cwd, project[1] || project[2]) : cwd;
  const visited = new Set();
  function measures(resource) {
    if (visited.has(resource)) return false;
    visited.add(resource);
    if (visited.size > 256) throw new Error('GPU resource scan too large; use PC_GPU_MODE=solo');
    let source;
    try { source = fs.readFileSync(resource.startsWith('res://') ? path.join(base, resource.slice(6)) : path.resolve(cwd, resource), 'utf8'); }
    catch (e) { if (e.code === 'ENOENT') return false; throw e; }
    source = source.replace(/^\s*#.*$/gm, '');
    if (/TIME_(?:PROCESS|FPS)|get_frame_setup_time_cpu|get_ticks_usec|frame[_ ]?(?:ms|times?)|gpu_ms|percentile|median/i.test(source)) return true;
    // Follow scene attachments, not every production preload in a capture script.
    // Effects use microsecond clocks for animation even when no speed is measured.
    return resource.endsWith('.tscn') && [...source.matchAll(/res:\/\/[^"'\s)]+\.(?:gd|tscn)/g)].some(m => measures(m[0]));
  }
  const resources = [...args.matchAll(/res:\/\/[^"'\s]+\.(?:gd|tscn)/g)].map(m => m[0]);
  const script = /--script\s+(?:"([^"]+)"|(\S+))/.exec(args);
  if (script) resources.push(script[1] || script[2]);
  if (!resources.length) {
    try {
      const config = fs.readFileSync(path.join(base, 'project.godot'), 'utf8');
      const main = /^run\/main_scene="(res:\/\/[^"\r\n]+)"/m.exec(config);
      if (main) resources.push(main[1]);
    } catch (e) { if (e.code !== 'ENOENT') throw e; }
  }
  return resources.some(measures) ? 'solo' : 'shared';
}

// Lanes C (board_c) and D (board_d) are code only (R268, R301). The runner flag or the working folder is enough (issue 947).
function codeOnlyLane(flag, cwd) {
  if (flag === 'C' || flag === 'D') return flag;
  const m = /(?:^|[\\/])board_([cd])(?:[\\/]|$)/i.exec(cwd || '');
  return m ? m[1].toUpperCase() : '';
}

async function main() {
  const [action, root, issue, pid, mode, weight = '1'] = process.argv.slice(2);
  if (action === 'classify') {
    const input = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(0, 'utf8').replace(/^\uFEFF/, ''));
    const mode = modeFor(input.exe, input.args, input.cwd, input.requested);
    console.log(JSON.stringify({ mode, weight: mode ? weightFor(input.exe, input.weight) : 1 }));
    return;
  }
  if (!root) throw new Error('GPU root is required');
  const slots = new Slots(root);
  if (action === 'release') { await transaction(() => slots.release(issue)); return; }
  if (action !== 'acquire' || !['shared', 'solo'].includes(mode)) throw new Error('Usage: gpu_slots.js acquire ROOT ISSUE PID shared|solo [WEIGHT]');
  const lane = codeOnlyLane(process.env.PC_GPU_LANE, process.cwd());
  if (lane) throw new Error(`Lane ${lane} is code only; GPU work refused`);
  const holder = { token: crypto.randomUUID(), issue, pid: Number(pid), mode,
    weight: positiveInteger(weight, 'GPU weight'), started: new Date().toISOString() };
  const waitSeconds = positiveInteger(process.env.PC_GPU_WAIT_SECONDS ?? '300', 'PC_GPU_WAIT_SECONDS');
  if (waitSeconds > MAX_WAIT_SECONDS) throw new Error(`PC_GPU_WAIT_SECONDS must be at most ${MAX_WAIT_SECONDS}`);
  slots.checkWeight(holder); // Fail even if the go gate is closed or the slot lock is busy.
  let cancelled = false;
  const cancel = () => { cancelled = true; };
  process.on('SIGINT', cancel);
  process.on('SIGTERM', cancel);
  try {
    await admit(slots, holder, { waitSeconds, cancelled: () => cancelled, onWait: () => console.log('WAIT') });
    console.log(holder.token);
  } finally {
    process.off('SIGINT', cancel);
    process.off('SIGTERM', cancel);
  }
}

// Wait for a claim until waitSeconds pass. Resolves with the holder once it is written; throws "admission timed out" or
// "admission cancelled" (owner exited, or cancelled() turned true) and then releases anything it reserved. tx and clock are
// for tests: tx defaults to the localhost lock, clock to the real one.
async function admit(slots, holder, { waitSeconds, cancelled = () => false, onWait = () => {}, waiterPid = process.pid,
  clock = realClock, tx = transaction } = {}) {
  const deadline = clock.now() + waitSeconds * 1000;
  let handedOff = false, touched = false;
  const check = () => {
    if (cancelled() || !slots.isAlive(holder.pid)) throw new Error('GPU admission cancelled');
    if (clock.now() >= deadline) throw new Error(`GPU admission timed out after ${waitSeconds} seconds`);
  };
  let heartbeat = clock.now();
  try {
    for (;;) {
      const claim = await tx(() => { touched = true; return slots.step(holder, waiterPid); }, check, deadline, clock);
      check();
      if (claim) { handedOff = true; return claim; }
      if (clock.now() - heartbeat >= 60000) { onWait(); heartbeat = clock.now(); }
      await clock.sleep(Math.min(250, Math.max(0, deadline - clock.now())));
    }
  } finally {
    try {
      if (!handedOff && touched) await tx(() => slots.release(holder.token), undefined, undefined, clock);
    } catch (e) { console.error('GPU claim cleanup: ' + e.message); }
  }
}
if (require.main === module) main().catch(e => { console.error('GPU claim: ' + e.message); process.exitCode = 1; });
module.exports = { Slots, modeFor, weightFor, transaction, codeOnlyLane, admit, MAX_WAIT_SECONDS };

# Run a program with a ceiling (PC watchdog). Agent runs also stop when progress stalls (issue 818).
# The program's output goes to this window (so "tmo.bat ... > log.txt 2>&1" captures it).
# Call it through tools\pc\tmo.bat <seconds> <exe> [args ...]: the bat hands the whole command line over in TMO_ARGS, so
# arguments that start with "-" or "--" reach the program untouched (PowerShell would read them as its own parameters).
param([switch]$LibraryOnly)
$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'

# runner_stall_tests.js runs this decision with fake clocks on Linux CI and probes real processes on Windows.
function Get-StopReason($Elapsed, $Idle, $Ceiling, $StallMinutes) {
  if ($Elapsed -ge $Ceiling) { return 'CEILING' }
  if ($Idle -ge ($StallMinutes * 60)) { return "STALLED after $StallMinutes min" }
  return ''
}
function Get-RunProgress($Repo, $Logs) {
  $files = @{}
  $dirs = New-Object 'System.Collections.Generic.Stack[string]'
  $dirs.Push($Repo)
  while ($dirs.Count) {
    foreach ($f in (Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $dirs.Pop() -Force -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue)) {
      # Reading git status may touch its index; Git housekeeping must not keep a silent agent alive.
      # Do not follow junctions outside this run's checkout.
      if ($f.Name -eq '.git' -or ($f.Attributes -band [IO.FileAttributes]::ReparsePoint)) { continue }
      if ($f.PSIsContainer) { $dirs.Push($f.FullName) }
      else { $files[$f.FullName] = "$($f.LastWriteTimeUtc.Ticks):$($f.Length)" }
    }
  }
  $head = (& git.exe -C $Repo rev-parse --verify HEAD 2>$null) -join ''
  $bytes = @{}
  foreach ($path in $Logs) {
    $f = Get-Item -LiteralPath $path -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
    $bytes[$path] = if ($f) { $f.Length } else { 0 }
  }
  return @{ Files = $files; Head = $head; Bytes = $bytes }
}
function Test-RunProgress($Before, $After) {
  if ($After.Head -and $After.Head -ne $Before.Head) { return $true }
  if ($After.Files.Count -ne $Before.Files.Count) { return $true }
  foreach ($key in $After.Files.Keys) {
    if (-not $Before.Files.ContainsKey($key) -or $Before.Files[$key] -ne $After.Files[$key]) { return $true }
  }
  foreach ($key in $After.Bytes.Keys) {
    if ($After.Bytes[$key] -gt $Before.Bytes[$key]) { return $true }
  }
  return $false
}
function Save-RunResume($Repo, $Out, $Logs, $Reason, $Base) {
  $utf8 = New-Object Text.UTF8Encoding($false)
  $changed = @(& git.exe --no-optional-locks -C $Repo -c core.quotepath=off diff --name-only $Base -- 2>$null)
  $changed += @(& git.exe --no-optional-locks -C $Repo -c core.quotepath=off ls-files --others --exclude-standard 2>$null)
  $changed = @($changed | Sort-Object -Unique)
  $note = "## Runner resume note (issue 818)`nStop reason: $Reason`n`nChanged files (from run base $Base):`n"
  if ($changed.Count) { $note += ($changed | ForEach-Object { "- $_" }) -join "`n" }
  else { $note += '(none)' }
  foreach ($path in $Logs) {
    $note += "`n`nLast log lines ($([IO.Path]::GetFileName($path))):`n"
    if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $path) {
      $tail = @(Get-Content -LiteralPath $path -Tail 12 -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | ForEach-Object {
        $line = [string]$_; if ($line.Length -gt 400) { $line = $line.Substring(0, 400) + ' [cut]' }; $line
      })
      $note += $tail -join "`n"
    } else { $note += '(no output)' }
  }
  $note += "`n## End runner resume note`n"
  [IO.File]::WriteAllText((Join-Path $Out 'resume.txt'), $note, $utf8)
  [IO.File]::WriteAllText((Join-Path $Out 'stop_reason.txt'), $Reason, $utf8)
}
# taskkill /T uses process enumeration that can be denied in the Codex sandbox, even for our own children.
# Toolhelp needs no WMI access. Keep process handles, not just PIDs, so a reused PID can never target another job.
function Initialize-RunTree {
  if ('RunnerProcessSnapshot' -as [type]) { return }
  Add-Type -TypeDefinition @'
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.ComponentModel;
using System.Runtime.InteropServices;
public static class RunnerProcessSnapshot {
  [StructLayout(LayoutKind.Sequential, CharSet = CharSet.Unicode)]
  struct Entry {
    public uint size, usage, id; public UIntPtr heap; public uint module, threads, parent;
    public int priority; public uint flags;
    [MarshalAs(UnmanagedType.ByValTStr, SizeConst = 260)] public string exe;
  }
  [DllImport("kernel32.dll", SetLastError = true)] static extern IntPtr CreateToolhelp32Snapshot(uint flags, uint id);
  [DllImport("kernel32.dll", CharSet = CharSet.Unicode, SetLastError = true)] static extern bool Process32FirstW(IntPtr h, ref Entry e);
  [DllImport("kernel32.dll", CharSet = CharSet.Unicode, SetLastError = true)] static extern bool Process32NextW(IntPtr h, ref Entry e);
  [DllImport("kernel32.dll")] static extern bool CloseHandle(IntPtr h);
  public static Dictionary<int, int> Parents() {
    IntPtr h = CreateToolhelp32Snapshot(2, 0);
    if (h == new IntPtr(-1)) throw new Win32Exception();
    try {
      var result = new Dictionary<int, int>();
      var e = new Entry(); e.size = (uint)Marshal.SizeOf(typeof(Entry));
      if (!Process32FirstW(h, ref e)) throw new Win32Exception();
      do { result[(int)e.id] = (int)e.parent; } while (Process32NextW(h, ref e));
      if (Marshal.GetLastWin32Error() != 18) throw new Win32Exception();
      return result;
    } finally { CloseHandle(h); }
  }
}
'@
}
function Update-RunTree($Tree) {
  $parents = [RunnerProcessSnapshot]::Parents()
  do {
    $added = $false
    foreach ($childId in $parents.Keys) {
      $parentId = $parents[$childId]
      if ($Tree.ContainsKey($childId) -or -not $Tree.ContainsKey($parentId)) { continue }
      $parent = $Tree[$parentId]
      # A retained handle keeps the original process identity after exit. StartTime also rejects reused parent PIDs.
      try {
        $child = [Diagnostics.Process]::GetProcessById($childId)
        $null = $child.Handle
        if ($child.StartTime -lt $parent.StartTime) { $child.Dispose(); continue }
        if ($parent.HasExited -and $child.StartTime -gt $parent.ExitTime) { $child.Dispose(); continue }
        $Tree[$childId] = $child; $added = $true
      } catch [ArgumentException] { } # The child already exited between snapshot and open.
    }
  } while ($added)
}
function Write-KillLog($Log, $Line) {
  Write-Output "run_timeout: $Line"
  try { "$(Get-Date -Format s) $Line" | Out-File -LiteralPath $Log -Append -Encoding utf8 }
  catch { Write-Output "run_timeout: could not append kill log: $($_.Exception.Message)" }
}
function Stop-RunTree($Process, $Tree, $Log) {
  Update-RunTree $Tree
  $ea = $ErrorActionPreference
  $killer = 'taskkill.exe'
  if ($env:PC_RUNNER_TEST -eq '1' -and $env:PC_TEST_TASKKILL) { $killer = $env:PC_TEST_TASKKILL }
  try {
    $ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue'
    $reply = @(& $killer /PID $Process.Id /T /F 2>&1)
    $code = $LASTEXITCODE
  } finally { $ErrorActionPreference = $ea }
  Write-KillLog $Log "taskkill PID $($Process.Id) exit ${code}: $($reply -join ' ')"
  $deadline = [DateTime]::UtcNow.AddSeconds(15)
  do {
    # Discover children before stopping their parents, and repeat in case one spawned during taskkill.
    Update-RunTree $Tree
    $live = @($Tree.Values | Where-Object { -not $_.HasExited } | Sort-Object StartTime -Descending)
    foreach ($child in $live) {
      try { $child.Kill() }
      catch { if (-not $child.HasExited) { Write-KillLog $Log "handle kill PID $($child.Id) failed: $($_.Exception.Message)" } }
    }
    foreach ($child in $live) { $null = $child.WaitForExit(100) }
    Update-RunTree $Tree
    $live = @($Tree.Values | Where-Object { -not $_.HasExited })
    if (-not $live.Count) {
      Write-KillLog $Log "kill verified: all $($Tree.Count) processes gone (PIDs $((@($Tree.Keys) | Sort-Object) -join ', '))"
      return
    }
  } while ([DateTime]::UtcNow -lt $deadline)
  $failure = "KILL FAILED: surviving PIDs $(($live | ForEach-Object { $_.Id }) -join ', '); checkout kept"
  Write-KillLog $Log $failure
  throw $failure
}
if ($LibraryOnly) { return }

}
# Nov 1: a test job ran all through Ryan's first Godot playtest, slowing the PC and overwriting the game's log.
# Wait while Ryan is playing: the game is Voidsworn.exe or a windowed Godot started with no job arguments
# (play_godot.bat passes none; every job passes --headless, --script, --bot or similar). Waiting is not timed.
function Test-RyanPlaying {
  try { $ps = Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "Name LIKE 'Voidsworn%.exe' OR Name LIKE 'Godot%.exe'" } catch { return $false }
  foreach ($q in $ps) {
    if ($q.Name -like '*_console.exe') { continue }
    $cl = [string]$q.CommandLine
    $rest = [regex]::Replace($cl, '^\s*("[^"]*"|\S+)', '')
    $rest = [regex]::Replace($rest, '--path\s+("[^"]*"|\S+)', '')
    if ($rest -notmatch '--?\w') { return $true }
  }
  return $false
}
$w0 = Get-Date; $said = $false

  Start-Sleep -Seconds 20
}

# A pending solo claim drains all shared slots and blocks new workers.

$classify = @{ exe = $Exe; args = $argLine; cwd = (Get-Location).Path; requested = [string]$env:PC_GPU_MODE; weight = [string]$env:PC_GPU_WEIGHT } | ConvertTo-Json -Compress
$gpuClass = ($classify | & $gpuNode $gpuHelper classify) -join ''
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw 'GPU command classification failed' }
$gpuClass = $gpuClass | ConvertFrom-Json
$gpuMode = $gpuClass.mode
if ($gpuMode) {
  Write-Output "GPU: waiting for $gpuMode claim, weight $($gpuClass.weight) (issue $env:PC_GPU_ISSUE)"
  # Admission has its own clock in gpu_slots.js (PC_GPU_WAIT_SECONDS, default 300).
  # Read the helper's diagnostic even under Windows PowerShell's native stderr handling.
  $ea = $ErrorActionPreference
  try {
    $ErrorActionPreference = 'Continue'
    & $gpuNode $gpuHelper acquire $gpuRoot ([string]$env:PC_GPU_ISSUE + ':gpu') $PID $gpuMode $gpuClass.weight 2>&1 | ForEach-Object {
      $line = [string]$_
      if ($line -eq 'WAIT') { Write-Host "GPU: still waiting for $gpuMode claim" }
      elseif ($line -cmatch '\A[0-9a-f]{8}(?:-[0-9a-f]{4}){3}-[0-9a-f]{12}\z') { $gpuToken = $line }
      else { Write-Output $line }
    }
    $admissionExit = $LASTEXITCODE
  } finally { $ErrorActionPreference = $ea }
  if ($admissionExit -ne 0 -or -not $gpuToken) {
    Write-Output 'GPU: admission failed; command was not started'
    exit 126
  }
  Write-Output "GPU: acquired $gpuMode claim $gpuToken"
}
# A forced claim applies to this command only, never a nested watchdog.
$env:PC_GPU_MODE = ''
$env:PC_GPU_WEIGHT = ''
Initialize-RunTree
$timer = [Diagnostics.Stopwatch]::StartNew()
$lastProgress = 0.0
$scanEvery = 60.0
if ($env:PC_RUNNER_TEST -eq '1' -and $env:PC_TEST_SCAN_S) { $scanEvery = [double]$env:PC_TEST_SCAN_S }
$nextScan = $scanEvery
# Child commands may use tmo.bat too. Their own timeout must never overwrite this agent's resume note.
$env:TMO_REPO = ''; $env:TMO_OUT = ''; $env:TMO_LOG = ''; $env:TMO_ERR_LOG = ''
try {
  if ($argLine -ne '') { $p = Start-Process -FilePath $Exe -ArgumentList $argLine -NoNewWindow -PassThru }
  else { $p = Start-Process -FilePath $Exe -NoNewWindow -PassThru }
} catch {
  "$(Get-Date -Format s) START FAILED $Exe $argLine : $_" | Out-File $log -Append -Encoding utf8
  Write-Output "run_timeout: could not start $Exe : $_"
  exit 125
}
$null = $p.Handle   # keeps the exit code readable after the process ends
$tree = @{ }; $tree[$p.Id] = $p
$completed = $false
try {
$poll = 5000
if ($env:PC_RUNNER_TEST -eq '1' -and $env:PC_TEST_POLL_MS) { $poll = [int]$env:PC_TEST_POLL_MS }
while ($true) {
  Update-RunTree $tree
  $remaining = [Math]::Max(1, [Math]::Min($poll, ($Seconds - $timer.Elapsed.TotalSeconds) * 1000))
  if ($p.WaitForExit([int]$remaining)) { $p.WaitForExit(); $completed = $true; exit $p.ExitCode }
  if ($watch) {
    # Scan the checkout once a minute, not every poll: a full walk of a big checkout every 5 s would load Ryan's PC for hours,
    # and a 30 minute stall window does not need finer steps (Board review, Nov 15).
    if ($timer.Elapsed.TotalSeconds -ge $nextScan) {
      $after = Get-RunProgress $watchRepo $logs
      if (Test-RunProgress $before $after) { $lastProgress = $timer.Elapsed.TotalSeconds }
      $before = $after
      $nextScan = $timer.Elapsed.TotalSeconds + $scanEvery
    }
    $reason = Get-StopReason $timer.Elapsed.TotalSeconds ($timer.Elapsed.TotalSeconds - $lastProgress) $Seconds $stall
  } else {
    $reason = if ($timer.Elapsed.TotalSeconds -ge $Seconds) { 'CEILING' } else { '' }
  }
  if ($p.HasExited) { $p.WaitForExit(); $completed = $true; exit $p.ExitCode }
  if ($reason) { break }
}
$secs = [int]$timer.Elapsed.TotalSeconds
Stop-RunTree $p $tree $log
if ($watch) { Save-RunResume $watchRepo $watchOut $logs $reason $base }
"$(Get-Date -Format s) TIMEOUT $reason after $secs s (ceiling $Seconds s), verified dead tree of PID $($p.Id): $Exe $argLine" | Out-File $log -Append -Encoding utf8
Write-Output "run_timeout: TIMEOUT $reason after $secs s, killed $Exe (PID $($p.Id)) and its children"
exit 124
} finally {
  # Reap on an unexpected watchdog error too. A successful queue launcher may deliberately start background jobs.
  if (-not $completed) {
    Update-RunTree $tree
    if (@($tree.Values | Where-Object { -not $_.HasExited }).Count) { Stop-RunTree $p $tree $log }
  }
  foreach ($child in $tree.Values) { $child.Dispose() }
}
} finally {
  if ($gpuToken) {
    & $gpuNode $gpuHelper release $gpuRoot $gpuToken
    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { Write-Output "GPU: release failed; dead owner PID $PID will be reclaimed" }
  }
}

@echo off
rem Run a program with a time limit: tmo.bat <seconds> <exe> [args ...]
rem Kills the whole process tree if it overruns (exit code 124, logged to Downloads\voidsworn_test\queue\logs\timeouts.log);
rem otherwise returns the program's own exit code. Its output comes out here, so "tmo.bat ... > log.txt 2>&1" captures it.
rem For agent runs set TMO_REPO, TMO_OUT and TMO_LOG. The seconds argument is then the hard ceiling (runners default
rem to 21600), and STALL_MIN (default 30) stops a run only after no file, commit or log progress. TMO_ERR_LOG is optional.
rem A stopped agent writes resume.txt and stop_reason.txt in TMO_OUT. Clear these TMO variables before runner checks.
rem R293: shared capacity is in Downloads\voidsworn_test\gpu_slots.json, e.g. {"sharedSlots":4}, default 2.
rem Godot GPU commands weigh 1, Blender commands weigh 2; PC_GPU_WEIGHT overrides the weight for this command.
rem Timing scripts take solo and wait for all slots, whatever the shared capacity.
rem For other GPU executables set PC_GPU_MODE=shared, or solo for timing. Waiting does not spend the command's limit.
setlocal
set "TMO_ARGS=%*"
powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "%~dp0run_timeout.ps1"
exit /b %ERRORLEVEL%

const defaultStall = Number(source.match(/\$stall = (\d+(?:\.\d+)?)/)[1]);
assert.equal(defaultStall, 30);
const decide = new Function('Elapsed', 'Idle', 'Ceiling', 'StallMinutes', body
  .replace(/\$(Elapsed|Idle|Ceiling|StallMinutes)\b/g, '$1').replace(/ -ge /g, ' >= ')
  .replace('"STALLED after StallMinutes min"', '`STALLED after ${StallMinutes} min`'));
let passed = 0;
function pass(name) { passed++; console.log('PASS ' + name); }
function fakeRun(progressEvery, ceiling = 21600, stallMinutes = defaultStall) {
  let last = 0;
  for (let now = 0; now <= ceiling; now += 60) {
    if (progressEvery && now % progressEvery === 0) last = now;
    const reason = decide(now, now - last, ceiling, stallMinutes);
    if (reason) return { now, reason };
    if (now === 7260) assert.equal(reason, '', 'a busy run past two hours must continue');
  }
  throw new Error('no stop at ceiling');
}
assert.deepEqual(fakeRun(60), { now: 21600, reason: 'CEILING' });
pass('busy fake run passes two hours and stops only at six-hour ceiling');
assert.deepEqual(fakeRun(0), { now: 1800, reason: 'STALLED after 30 min' });
pass('silent fake run stops at the stall window');
assert.deepEqual(fakeRun(60, 10800), { now: 10800, reason: 'CEILING' });
assert.deepEqual(fakeRun(0, 21600, 10), { now: 600, reason: 'STALLED after 10 min' });
assert.equal(decide(21600, 1800, 21600, 30), 'CEILING');
assert.equal(decide(1799, 1799, 21600, 30), '');
pass('ceiling and stall overrides, exact boundaries, ceiling takes priority');

      PC_TEST_TASKKILL: ['busy', 'lying-killer'].includes(scenario) ? killer : '' },
    stdio: ['ignore', fd, fd], windowsHide: true, timeout: 30000
  }); } finally { fs.closeSync(fd); }
  helpers.push(run.pid);
  const output = fs.readFileSync(log, 'utf8');

  assert.equal(pids.length, ['busy', 'launcher-exit'].includes(scenario) ? 3 : 1);
  if (scenario === 'launcher-exit') {
    assert(!alive(pids[0]) && pids.slice(1).every(alive), 'ordinary queue launchers must be able to leave background work running');
    for (const pid of pids.slice(1).reverse()) process.kill(pid, 'SIGKILL');
    const deadline = Date.now() + 5000;
    while (pids.some(alive) && Date.now() < deadline) Atomics.wait(new Int32Array(new SharedArrayBuffer(4)), 0, 0, 50);
    assert(pids.every(pid => !alive(pid)), 'background fixture cleanup failed');
    pass('normal launcher keeps exit 7 and background jobs; the test reaps its child and grandchild'); continue;

  assert(pids.every(pid => !alive(pid)), scenario + ': fake agents survived: ' + pids);
  const verified = output.match(/kill verified: all (\d+) processes gone \(PIDs ([\d, ]+)\)/);

  if (scenario === 'lying-killer') assert(/taskkill PID \d+ exit 0:/.test(output), output);

  const note = fs.readFileSync(path.join(folder, 'resume.txt'), 'utf8');
  assert(note.includes(reason) && note.includes(busy ? 'still working' : 'last action: waiting'));
  pass(`Windows ${scenario}: verifies every agent is gone, saves reason and tail, logs taskkill and fallback result`);
}
const timeoutLog = fs.readFileSync(path.join(root, 'timeouts.log'), 'utf8');
assert(timeoutLog.includes('CEILING') && timeoutLog.includes('STALLED after 0.02 min'));

helpers.push(normal.pid);
pass('ordinary commands keep their exit code and explicit limit, child commands cannot inherit the agent watch');
} finally {
  for (const pid of [...agentPids().reverse(), ...helpers].filter(Boolean)) {
    if (alive(pid)) process.kill(pid, 'SIGKILL');
  }
  // Wait for forced cleanup on failure too. Never trust a kill's return value alone.
  const deadline = Date.now() + 5000;
  while (agentPids().some(alive) && Date.now() < deadline) Atomics.wait(new Int32Array(new SharedArrayBuffer(4)), 0, 0, 50);
  assert(agentPids().every(pid => !alive(pid)), 'test cleanup leaked fake agents');

pass('final cleanup asserts none of this test run\'s fake agents is alive');

3.4 **GPU work is sized to the job and to what the RTX 5080 can hold, not to a fixed job count; frame-time measurements run
solo. Lanes C and D stay code only.** The Board decides per job how many share the card: light captures and probes can run side by
side, heavy bakes and Blender renders take more of it, and nothing is started that would run the card out of its 16 GB of
graphics memory. The launchers take and release claims themselves through tools/pc/gpu_slots.js (shared capacity from
Downloads\voidsworn_test\gpu_slots.json, {"sharedSlots":2} by default, raised by the Board; captures weigh 1, Blender and heavy
bakes 2, with PC_GPU_WEIGHT for the job's chosen weight), or a solo claim that waits for every slot
to empty and blocks new GPU work while it measures. Dead process claims are reclaimed. `gpu_go.txt` still wins: no new GPU
work starts unless its first line is `GPU cleared` (optionally followed by `: reason`); the gaming pause in 3.11 is
unchanged. Why: "that one GPU job at a time is an outdated rule, we should be flexible based on the task and what my
graphicsa card can actually handle as a 5080. we don't want to overwhelm it but we don't want to artificially restrict small
jobs and extend things out" (Ryan, Nov 16, R293). Solo measurements keep speed numbers honest. Replaces the two-job cap
(R276, Nov 15) and the one-job limit before it (R173b, effects-09); the gaming pause (R173) remains. Set: Ryan, Nov 16 (R293).

3.5 **Every PC queue job is wrapped in the tmo.bat watchdog, ends with exit /b, and carries no |, &, < or > in its
arguments.** Agent runs stop after STALL_MIN minutes without progress (default 30), or at a hard ceiling (default six
hours; CODEX_CAP and WORK_CAP override the ceiling in seconds). Progress is a changed file write time in the run repo,
a new commit, or new bytes in log.txt (also stderr for workhorse runs). Git housekeeping does not count. A stall or ceiling
stop writes its reason, changed files and last log lines to the out folder's resume.txt and the -partial commit message;
the next run reads that note. timeouts.log records STALLED after N min or CEILING. Short queue commands and runner checks
keep their explicit time limits. Why: hung Godot windows blocked the queue for about three hours (G-3), but the old two-hour
agent cap killed jobs that were still working (367 and 371). Ryan, Nov 15: "do we need to get rid of that random 2 hour rule
if it's timing out actual work?" He took up a stall check, a high backstop and a saved resume note (issue 818). Set: Board
(G-3), Ryan (issue 818). Ids: G-3, feel-06, skill-pb-05.

3.6 **The Board may double-click .bat files on the PC itself by computer use, a few clicks at a time, releasing the lock at
once, never while Ryan is playing; it never drives the PC by remote clicks to unstick a job.** Why: "can't you use computer
use for all of this?" (R126); holding the lock hides his windows; Task Manager ignores remote clicks. Set: Ryan (R126); Board
(the limits). Ids: R126a, R126b, loop-16, skill-pb-06.

3.7 **Clone on the PC with `git -c core.autocrlf=false`.** Why: CRLF breaks the campaign table export. Set: Board. Id: loop-14.

3.8 **Free tools and sources, local first; a paid service only with Ryan's yes, for that one evaluation.** Why: money is his.
Set: Ryan (AGENTS.md; R110). Ids: R110b, agents-20, audit-02.

'use strict';
// Issue 818: fake clocks run on Linux CI; Windows also exercises the real watchdog and resume path for free.
const assert = require('assert');
const fs = require('fs');

// Run the actual small PowerShell decision on every CI host. Only its arithmetic and variable syntax need translation.
const body = source.match(/function Get-StopReason\([^\n]+\) \{([\s\S]*?)\n\}/)[1];
const defaultStall = Number(source.match(/\$stall = (\d+(?:\.\d+)?)/)[1]);
assert.equal(defaultStall, 30);
const decide = new Function('Elapsed', 'Idle', 'Ceiling', 'StallMinutes', body
  .replace(/\$(Elapsed|Idle|Ceiling|StallMinutes)\b/g, '$1').replace(/ -ge /g, ' >= ')
  .replace('"STALLED after StallMinutes min"', '`STALLED after ${StallMinutes} min`'));
let passed = 0;
function pass(name) { passed++; console.log('PASS ' + name); }
function fakeRun(progressEvery, ceiling = 21600, stallMinutes = defaultStall) {
  let last = 0;
  for (let now = 0; now <= ceiling; now += 60) {
    if (progressEvery && now % progressEvery === 0) last = now;
    const reason = decide(now, now - last, ceiling, stallMinutes);
    if (reason) return { now, reason };
    if (now === 7260) assert.equal(reason, '', 'a busy run past two hours must continue');
  }
  throw new Error('no stop at ceiling');
}
assert.deepEqual(fakeRun(60), { now: 21600, reason: 'CEILING' });
pass('busy fake run passes two hours and stops only at six-hour ceiling');
assert.deepEqual(fakeRun(0), { now: 1800, reason: 'STALLED after 30 min' });
pass('silent fake run stops at the stall window');
assert.deepEqual(fakeRun(60, 10800), { now: 10800, reason: 'CEILING' });
assert.deepEqual(fakeRun(0, 21600, 10), { now: 600, reason: 'STALLED after 10 min' });
assert.equal(decide(21600, 1800, 21600, 30), 'CEILING');
assert.equal(decide(1799, 1799, 21600, 30), '');
pass('ceiling and stall overrides, exact boundaries, ceiling takes priority');

  const verified = output.match(/kill verified: all (\d+) processes gone \(PIDs ([\d, ]+)\)/);
  assert(verified && Number(verified[1]) >= pids.length && pids.every(pid => verified[2].split(', ').map(Number).includes(pid)), output);
  if (busy) assert(/taskkill PID \d+ exit 5:/.test(output) && output.includes('Access denied'), output);
  if (scenario === 'lying-killer') assert(/taskkill PID \d+ exit 0:/.test(output), output);
  assert.equal(fs.readFileSync(path.join(folder, 'stop_reason.txt'), 'utf8'), reason);
  const note = fs.readFileSync(path.join(folder, 'resume.txt'), 'utf8');
  assert(note.includes(reason) && note.includes(busy ? 'still working' : 'last action: waiting'));
  pass(`Windows ${scenario}: verifies every agent is gone, saves reason and tail, logs taskkill and fallback result`);
}
const timeoutLog = fs.readFileSync(path.join(root, 'timeouts.log'), 'utf8');
assert(timeoutLog.includes('CEILING') && timeoutLog.includes('STALLED after 0.02 min'));
const ordinary = path.join(root, 'ordinary.js');
fs.writeFileSync(ordinary, 'setTimeout(()=>process.exit(7),500);');
const normal = spawnSync('powershell.exe', ['-NoProfile', '-ExecutionPolicy', 'Bypass', '-File', path.join(pc, 'run_timeout.ps1')], {
  env: { ...process.env, TMO_ARGS: `4 "${process.execPath}" "${ordinary}"`, TMO_REPO: '', TMO_OUT: '', TMO_LOG: '', TMO_ERR_LOG: '',
    STALL_MIN: '0.001', PC_RUNNER_TEST: '1', PC_TEST_POLL_MS: '100', PC_TEST_SCAN_S: '0.1', PC_TEST_TIMEOUT_LOG: path.join(root, 'timeouts.log') },
  encoding: 'utf8', windowsHide: true, timeout: 15000
});
assert.equal(normal.status, 7, normal.stdout + normal.stderr);
helpers.push(normal.pid);
pass('ordinary commands keep their exit code and explicit limit, child commands cannot inherit the agent watch');
} finally {
  for (const pid of [...agentPids().reverse(), ...helpers].filter(Boolean)) {
    if (alive(pid)) process.kill(pid, 'SIGKILL');
  }
  // Wait for forced cleanup on failure too. Never trust a kill's return value alone.
  const deadline = Date.now() + 5000;
  while (agentPids().some(alive) && Date.now() < deadline) Atomics.wait(new Int32Array(new SharedArrayBuffer(4)), 0, 0, 50);
  assert(agentPids().every(pid => !alive(pid)), 'test cleanup leaked fake agents');
}
pass('final cleanup asserts none of this test run\'s fake agents is alive');
console.log('Evidence: ' + root);
console.log(`PASS runner stall checks (${passed} groups)`);
@echo off
rem Ship recipe: shared settings for every stage (called by the stage .bat files, not run on its own).
rem Asks for the ship id once (the ship file name in tools\recipe\ships without .json, for example C15s_23); Enter keeps C15s_23.
rem Every FOR /F line below wraps its whole command in one more pair of quotes: cmd strips the first and last quote of a
rem quoted command, so without them the python path and the script path break apart (issue 587, finding 1; issue 592).
set V=%USERPROFILE%\Downloads\voidsworn_test
set B=%V%\blender\blender-4.5.14-windows-x64\blender.exe
set BPY=%V%\blender\blender-4.5.14-windows-x64\4.5\python\bin\python.exe
set GD=%V%\godot\Godot_v4.7.2-stable_win64_console.exe
set TPY=%USERPROFILE%\Downloads\trellis2\code\venv\Scripts\python.exe
for %%I in ("%~dp0..\..") do set REPO=%%~fI
set R=%REPO%\tools\blender\r209
set RC=%REPO%\tools\recipe
if "%SHIP%"=="" set /p SHIP=Ship id (Enter for C15s_23): 
if "%SHIP%"=="" set SHIP=C15s_23
set JOB=%V%\recipe\%SHIP%
set B0=%V%\bakeoff\%SHIP%_trellis.glb
if not exist "%JOB%" mkdir "%JOB%"
rem Pillow and scipy in Blender's own python, before any stage uses it (stage 1's concept write needs Pillow). First run only.
"%BPY%" -c "import scipy, PIL" 2> nul || ("%BPY%" -m ensurepip & "%BPY%" -m pip install scipy pillow)
"%BPY%" -c "import scipy, PIL" 2> nul || echo WARNING: Pillow or scipy is still missing from Blender's python; stages 1, 3 and 4 will stop.
for /f "usebackq delims=" %%L in (`""%BPY%" "%RC%\ship.py" %SHIP% get hull"`) do set HULL=%%L
for /f "usebackq delims=" %%L in (`""%BPY%" "%RC%\ship.py" %SHIP% get cell"`) do set CELL=%%L
rem round 7: the bake builds with the ship's own hull module (bakeAb6.py reads R209_HULL); CODE is the ship folder's name in the game
set R209_HULL=%HULL%
set CODE=
for /f "usebackq delims=" %%L in (`""%BPY%" "%RC%\ship.py" %SHIP% get game_code"`) do set CODE=%%L
if "%CODE%"=="" set CODE=%SHIP%
set R209_SHIP=%RC%\ships\%SHIP%.json
set R209_TUNE_JSON=
for /f "usebackq delims=" %%L in (`""%BPY%" "%RC%\ship.py" %SHIP% get tune"`) do set R209_TUNE_JSON=%REPO%\%%L
if "%R209_TUNE_JSON%"=="" if exist "%JOB%\tune.json" set R209_TUNE_JSON=%JOB%\tune.json
rem the stage 5 bake script, chosen by the ship json ("bake"); bakeAb6.py builds whichever hull module HULL names, so it serves any
rem ship whose hull module follows armA5 to armA7; a ship with its own bake names it in its json
set BAKE=
for /f "usebackq delims=" %%L in (`""%BPY%" "%RC%\ship.py" %SHIP% get bake"`) do set BAKE=%%L
if "%BAKE%"=="" set BAKE=bakeAb6.py
rem issue 601: "stack" is the faction kit's paint stack for a ship built from a build sheet (bake_sheet.py with
rem tools\blender\kits\FACTION\stack.py). A faction whose kit has no stack yet takes the unpainted route ("none"), and the
rem stages say so (STACK_NOTE).
set FACTION=
for /f "usebackq delims=" %%L in (`""%BPY%" "%RC%\ship.py" %SHIP% get faction"`) do set FACTION=%%L
set STACK_NOTE=
if /i "%BAKE%"=="stack" if not exist "%REPO%\tools\blender\kits\%FACTION%\stack.py" set "STACK_NOTE=the %FACTION% kit has no paint stack yet (tools\blender\kits\%FACTION%\stack.py): unpainted"
if /i "%BAKE%"=="stack" if exist "%REPO%\tools\blender\kits\%FACTION%\stack.py" set BAKE=bake_sheet.py
if /i "%BAKE%"=="stack" set BAKE=none
rem issue 592: a ship built from a build sheet names it ("sheet"); its class length (LEN) is read from the sheet, else the json
set SHEET=
for /f "usebackq delims=" %%L in (`""%BPY%" "%RC%\ship.py" %SHIP% get sheet"`) do set SHEET=%%L
set LEN=
for /f "usebackq delims=" %%L in (`""%BPY%" "%RC%\ship.py" %SHIP% get length_world"`) do set LEN=%%L
rem issue 587: an empty HULL or CODE here means the lines above could not run ship.py; stop instead of building the wrong ship
if "%HULL%"=="" echo WARNING: ships\%SHIP%.json gave no hull module (or ship.py did not run); the stages that build will stop.
@echo off
rem Ship recipe, stage 1: concept in. Checks the concept image is in the repo (docs\ship_pipeline\concepts\SHIP.webp), starts the
rem ship's placement file (tools\recipe\ships\SHIP.json) from the template if there is none, makes the job folder
rem Downloads\voidsworn_test\recipe\SHIP\ and writes the concept there as a PNG with its outline (alpha).
rem A ship whose json already exists reads its concept from the json ("concept"): for a ship built from a build sheet that
